Experiments on AIME 2024, AIME 2025, and GAIA with Gemini-2.5-Flash-Lite and Qwen3.5-4B show that ExComm consistently outperforms strong test-time scaling baselines, achieving average performance gains of 5.7% and 5.0% over the best-performing baselines, respectively. Further analyses demonstrate improved error recovery, favorable scaling behavior, stronger diversity than adapted communication baselines, and the best performance-cost trade-off among the evaluated methods.

</details>


### [253] [ArborKV: Structure-Aware KV Cache Management for Scaling Tree-based LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22106)
*Yeqiu Chen,Ziyan Liu,Zhenxin Huang,Runquan Gui,Hong Wang,Lei Liu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: ArborKV addresses memory bottlenecks in Tree-of-Thoughts LLM reasoning by using a structure-aware eviction framework with value estimation and lazy rehydration, achieving ~4x KV-memory reduction while maintaining accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Tree-of-Thoughts (ToT) reasoning amplifies KV cache memory usage, causing memory bottlenecks that limit throughput, search depth, and width under fixed hardware budgets.

Method: ArborKV employs a lightweight value estimator, tree-aware allocation policy, token-extractive eviction, and lazy rehydration to support revisits.

Result: Up to ~4x peak KV-memory reduction while preserving near-full-retention accuracy.

Conclusion: ArborKV enables larger search configurations under fixed device budgets that would otherwise run out of memory.

Abstract: Recent progress in LLM reasoning has increasingly shifted from single-pass generation to explicit search over intermediate reasoning states. Tree-of-Thoughts (ToT) organizes inference to tree-structured search with branching and backtracking, but it substantially amplifies the Key--Value (KV) cache: retaining KV states for a frontier of partial trajectories quickly becomes a memory bottleneck that limits throughput and constrains search depth and width under fixed hardware budgets. We address this challenge by observing that KV reuse in ToT-style inference is governed by search dynamics: near-term decoding depends primarily on the active branch and its ancestors, whereas inactive subtrees have low short-term reuse probability yet must remain recoverable for backtracking. Motivated by this, we propose ArborKV, a structure-aware eviction framework that couples a lightweight value estimator with a tree-aware allocation policy, and performs purely token-extractive eviction with lazy rehydration to support revisits. Experiments on ToT-style reasoning benchmarks show that ArborKV achieves up to ~4x peak KV-memory reduction while preserving near-full-retention accuracy, enabling larger search configurations under fixed device budgets that would otherwise run out of memory.

</details>


### [254] [Ratchet: A Minimal Hygiene Recipe for Self-Evolving LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22148)
*Xing Zhang,Yanwei Cui,Guanghui Wang,Ziyuan Li,Wei Qiu,Bing Zhu,Peiyang He*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Ratchet通过单一LLM智能体循环（编写、检索、整理、退休技能）解决自进化技能库的生命周期管理问题，核心创新是outcome-driven retirement和meta-skill authoring。在MBPP+和SWE-bench上显著超越基线和消融变体，并从理论上证明了有界容量与退休阈值防止性能低于无技能基线。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 发现LLM编写的技能相比无技能基线无提升（+0.0pp），而人类策划的技能提升+16.2pp。这表明瓶颈不在于技能编写，而在于技能的全生命周期管理（创建、检索、整理、退休）。现有系统在技能管理机制上存在缺陷。

Method: Ratchet采用单一智能体循环架构，集成四种候选 hygiene 机制：(1) outcome-driven retirement 结果驱动退休机制，(2) bounded active-cap 有界活跃技能上限，(3) meta-skill authoring guidance 元技能编写指导，(4) pattern canonicalisation 模式规范化。在MBPP+ hard-100（Claude Opus 4.7）上评估，使用100轮3个随机种子的实验设置。

Result: 在MBPP+ hard-100上，Ratchet将pass@1从0.258±0.047基线提升至晚期滚动均值0.584（峰值0.658±0.042），滚动均值增益+0.328±0.018，而无技能对照组仅+0.002±0.005。在SWE-bench Verified上，20轮内峰值提升+0.22。8个消融实验(A1-A8)表明：退休机制和元技能编写先验是核心必要组件，显式去重机制可被元技能机制替代。

Conclusion: Ratchet通过单一智能体循环管理技能生命周期，在MBPP+上实现+32.8pp提升，在SWE-bench上实现+22pp提升。最小有效配方为：技能退休机制+元技能编写先验。去重和覆盖守卫等显式去重机制可被元技能机制替代。理论上证明了有界容量和退休阈值共同防止性能漂移低于无技能基线。

Abstract: Self-evolving skill libraries, pioneered by Voyager, let frozen LLM agents accumulate reusable knowledge without weight updates, yet recent evaluation shows that LLM-authored skills deliver $+0.0$pp over no-skill baselines while human-curated ones deliver $+16.2$pp: the bottleneck is not skill authoring but lifecycle management. We introduce \textbf{Ratchet}, a single-agent loop in which a frozen LLM writes, retrieves, curates, and retires its own natural-language skills. Ratchet integrates four candidate hygiene mechanisms: outcome-driven retirement, a bounded active-cap, meta-skill authoring guidance, and pattern canonicalisation. On MBPP+ hard-100 with Claude Opus 4.7, Ratchet lifts held-out pass@1 from a $0.258 \pm 0.047$ baseline to a late-window rolling mean of $0.584$ (peak $0.658 \pm 0.042$) across 100 rounds and 3 seeds, a $+0.328 \pm 0.018$ rolling-mean gain where the no-skill control drifts at $+0.002 \pm 0.005$; the same recipe transfers to an agentic solver on SWE-bench Verified ($+0.22$ peak lift over 20 rounds). Eight ablations (A1--A8) reveal that the minimal working recipe is smaller than our design suggests: retirement and the meta-skill authoring prior are load-bearing, while explicit deduplication (canonicalisation, cover-guard) is subsumed by the meta-skill itself. A non-divergence proposition shows that bounded cap and retirement threshold together prevent expected performance from drifting below the no-skills floor.

</details>


### [255] [ST-SimDiff: Balancing Spatiotemporal Similarity and Difference for Efficient Video Understanding with MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22158)
*Bingjun Luo,Tony Wang,Chaoqi Chen,Xinpeng Ding*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 本文提出ST-SimDiff框架，通过相似性识别冗余、差异性捕获关键事件的双视角处理长视频，在保留静态和动态内容的同时大幅降低计算开销，实验验证其性能优于现有最优方法。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有方法主要通过剪枝或合并token来减少冗余，但这些方法很大程度上忽略了一个关键维度——视频内容的变化和转折点，且缺乏对时空关系的协同建模能力。

Method: ST-SimDiff采用训练无关框架：首先从视觉token构建时空图以统一建模复杂关联；随后采用并行双选择策略——基于相似性的选择通过社区检测保留代表性token压缩静态信息；基于时序差异的选择精确定位内容变化点以保留关键动态变化的token。

Result: 实验表明，该方法在显著降低计算成本的同时，性能明显优于当前最优方法。

Conclusion: ST-SimDiff通过双选择策略有效平衡了静态信息压缩和动态事件保留，在显著降低计算成本的同时超越了现有最优方法。

Abstract: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) face significant computational overhead when processing long videos due to the massive number of visual tokens required. To improve efficiency, existing methods primarily reduce redundancy by pruning or merging tokens based on importance or similarity. However, these approaches largely overlook a critical dimension of video content, i.e., changes and turning points, and they lack a collaborative model for spatio-temporal relationships. To address this, we propose a new perspective: similarity is for identifying redundancy, while difference is for capturing key events. Based on this, we designed a training-free framework named ST-SimDiff. We first construct a spatio-temporal graph from the visual tokens to uniformly model their complex associations. Subsequently, we employ a parallel dual-selection strategy: 1) similarity-based selection uses community detection to retain representative tokens, compressing static information; 2) temporal difference-based selection precisely locates content-changing points to preserve tokens that capture key dynamic shifts. This allows it to preserve both static and dynamic content with a minimal number of tokens. Extensive experiments show our method significantly outperforms state-of-the-art approaches while substantially reducing computational costs. Our code is available in https://github.com/bingjunluo/ST-SimDiff.

</details>


### [256] [Adapting the Interface, Not the Model: Runtime Harness Adaptation for Deterministic LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22166)
*Tianshi Xu,Huifeng Wen,Meng Li*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Life-Harness通过运行时harness层面的适配改进冻结的LLM agents，无需修改模型权重，在多基准测试中展现强大的跨模型迁移性，平均提升88.5%，为模型中心化训练提供互补方案。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有agent适配方法主要更新模型参数，但在确定性、规则支配领域中，许多失败源于模型-环境接口的不匹配，而非模型本身的能力不足。

Method: 提出Life-Harness，一种生命周期感知的运行时harness，通过从训练轨迹中学习，将反复出现的交互失败转化为跨环境契约、程序技能、动作实现和轨迹调控的可复用干预措施，且在评估时保持固定。

Result: 在7个确定性环境中测试（来自τ-bench、τ²-bench和AgentBench），在18个模型后端的126个模型-环境设置中改善了116个，平均相对提升88.5%；从Qwen3-4B-Instruct轨迹演化的harness可迁移至其他17个模型。

Conclusion: Life-Harness将运行时接口适配定位为模型中心化agent训练的互补替代方案，证明了可迁移的环境侧结构可以被捕获和复用。

Abstract: LLM agents are shaped not only by their language models, but also by the runtime harness that mediates observation, tool use, action execution, feedback interpretation, and trajectory control. While existing agent adaptation methods mainly update model parameters, many failures in deterministic, rule-governed domains stem from mismatches at the model--environment interface. We propose Life-Harness, a lifecycle-aware runtime harness that improves frozen LLM agents without changing model weights or evaluation environments. Life-Harness evolves from training trajectories by converting recurring interaction failures into reusable interventions across environment contracts, procedural skills, action realization, and trajectory regulation, and remains fixed during held-out evaluation. On seven deterministic environments from $τ$-bench, $τ^2$-bench, and AgentBench, Life-Harness improves 116 out of 126 model--environment settings across 18 model backbones, with an average relative improvement of 88.5%. Harnesses evolved only from Qwen3-4B-Instruct trajectories transfer to 17 other models, showing that Life-Harness captures reusable environment-side structure rather than model-specific behavior. These results position runtime interface adaptation as a complementary alternative to model-centric agent training. Code is available at GitHub.

</details>


### [257] [Measuring Cross-Modal Synergy: A Benchmark for VLM Explainability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22168)
*Joël Roman Ky,Salah Ghamizi,Maxime Cordy*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: The paper identifies that current VLM explainability evaluation suffers from cross-modal redundancy issues where VLMs can answer visual questions using text alone, causing traditional unimodal metrics to fail. The authors propose Synergistic Faithfulness (F_syn) based on Shapley Interaction Index as a scalable, accurate, and computationally efficient solution (24× speedup, ρ=0.92). Evaluation across multiple methods, architectures, and datasets shows existing VLM explainers over-rely on visual features while adapted attention-based methods better capture cross-modal synergy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current post-hoc explainers for VLMs rely on unimodal perturbation metrics, but multimodal datasets contain language priors and modality biases. VLMs exhibit cross-modal redundancy—answering visual queries using text alone—which causes unimodal metrics to penalize faithful explainers, triggering evaluation collapse where visual and textual rankings contradict (Kendall's τ=-0.06).

Method: Synergistic Faithfulness uses Shapley Interaction Index to isolate joint Harsanyi dividend between modalities. This serves as an accurate surrogate (ρ=0.92) with 24× computational speedup compared to baselines.

Result: Evaluating 8 XAI methods across 3 VLM architectures and 3 datasets reveals that VLM-specific explainers over-index on visual salience and underperform adapted attention-based methods in capturing true cross-modal synergy.

Conclusion: Proposed Synergistic Faithfulness (F_syn) as a rigorous evaluation framework that can safely audit VLM reasoning in high-stakes deployments.

Abstract: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) map complex visual inputs to semantic spaces, but interpreting the cross-modal reasoning of VLMs currently relies on post-hoc explainers evaluated via unimodal perturbation metrics. We expose a limitation in this paradigm: because multimodal datasets contain language priors and modality biases, VLMs frequently exhibit cross-modal redundancy, allowing them to answer visual queries using text alone. Consequently, unimodal metrics penalize faithful explainers, triggering an evaluation collapse where visual and textual rankings fundamentally contradict each other. %(Kendall's $τ= -0.06$). To resolve this, we introduce Synergistic Faithfulness ($\mathcal{F}_{syn}$), a scalable metric rooted in the Shapley Interaction Index that strictly isolates the joint Harsanyi dividend between modalities, serving as a highly accurate surrogate ($ρ= 0.92$) while achieving a $24\times$ computational speedup. Evaluating 8 distinct XAI methods across 3 VLM architectures and 3 benchmark datasets, reveals that explainers proposed for VLMs heavily over-index on visual salience and significantly underperform adapted attention-based methods in capturing true cross-modal synergy. By decoupling visual plausibility from cross-modal faithfulness, this work provides a rigorous evaluation framework required to safely audit VLM reasoning in high-stakes deployments.

</details>


### [258] [LLM-Metrics: Measuring Research Impact Through Large Language Model Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22176)
*Si Shen,Wenhua Zhao,Danhao Zhu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: LLM-Metrics利用LLM参数记忆评估研究影响力，通过选择题探针测量模型对论文的"记忆"程度。核心假设是高影响力论文获得更多曝光，训练数据中暴露更多，从而在模型中形成更强记忆。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 当前引用指标存在时间延迟、学科偏差和马太效应等固有局限。

Method: 设计四类选择题探针（标题、作者、方法、venue识别），评估549篇2023-2024年CS论文在17个LLM（0.5B-72B参数）上的表现。

Result: 17个模型中15个产生正预测，9个在p<0.05水平显著；整体Spearman相关系数ρ=0.1495，p=0.0004；2024论文相关性更强（ρ=0.1880）；作者识别探针区分力最强；模型规模与预测能力非单调，3B参数的Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct表现优于多数更大模型（ρ=0.1829）。

Conclusion: LLM-Metrics提供了一种实时、跨学科、不依赖引用次数的研究评估新范式。模型规模与预测能力呈非单调关系，3B参数模型反而优于更大的模型，表明选择性记忆机制可能充当有效的信息过滤器。

Abstract: Citation counts remain the dominant metric for assessing research impact, yet they suffer from well-documented limitations: temporal lag, disciplinary bias, and Matthew effects. Here we propose LLM-Metrics, a research-impact assessment metric derived from the parametric memory of large language models (LLMs). The central hypothesis is that high-impact papers receive greater exposure in the academic community, that this exposure enters LLM training data in textual form, and that models consequently form stronger parametric memory of these papers. We designed four types of multiple-choice probes, covering title recognition, author recognition, method recognition, and venue recognition, and evaluated 549 computer science papers published in 2023-2024 across 17 LLMs spanning 0.5B to 72B parameters from six vendors. Of the 17 models, 15 produced positive predictions, 9 of which were significant at p less than 0.05, with an overall Spearman correlation of rho = 0.1495 and p = 0.0004 against citation counts. Three additional findings support the proposed mechanism. First, the predictive signal was stronger for 2024 papers, rho = 0.1880, whose citation counts were near zero at model-training time, reducing the plausibility of a simple reverse-causality explanation. Second, author-recognition probes showed the strongest discriminative power, consistent with an exposure-driven memory mechanism. Third, model scale and predictive power were non-monotonic: a 3B-parameter model, Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct, with rho = 0.1829, outperformed most larger models, supporting a selective-memory hypothesis in which the limited capacity of smaller models can serve as an effective information filter. LLM-Metrics offers a real-time, cross-disciplinary, citation-independent paradigm for research assessment.

</details>


### [259] [Skill Weaving: Efficient LLM Improvement via Modular Skillpacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22205)
*Zhuo Li,Guodong Du,Zesheng Shi,Weiyang Guo,Weijun Yao,Yuan Zhou,Jiabo Zhang,Jing Li*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: SkillWeave是一种模块化改进框架，通过将通用模型能力划分为可压缩的skillpacks，实现多域专业化和低延迟推理，在性能超越更大模型的同时显著降低计算成本


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 大型语言模型在跨领域专业化时，现有的方法难以平衡多域能力与严格的内存和推理约束

Method: SkillWeave将通用模型的完整能力划分为skillpacks（轻量级、领域特定的增量模块），用于重组和精炼模型内部知识；并集成SkillZip将skillpacks压缩为紧凑的推理就绪格式

Result: 9B参数的SkillWeave模型在多任务和智能体基准测试中优于多个基线模型，甚至超越32B单体LLM，同时实现最高4倍加速

Conclusion: SkillWeave能够在固定内存预算下实现强大的多域性能，支持低延迟执行

Abstract: Large language models increasingly require specialization across diverse domains, yet existing approaches struggle to balance multi-domain capacities with strict memory and inference constraints. In this work, we introduce SkillWeave, a modular improvement framework that enables LLMs to specialize under fixed memory budgets. SkillWeave partitions full capabilities of a general-purpose model into skillpacks -- lightweight, domain-specific delta modules -- that reorganize and refine the model's internal knowledge. For efficient deployment, SkillWeave integrates SkillZip to compress skillpacks into compact and inference-ready format, enabling strong multi-domain performance with low-latency execution. On multi-task and agentic benchmarks, a 9B SkillWeave model outperforms several baselines and even surpasses a 32B monolithic LLM, while achieving up to 4x speedup.

</details>


### [260] [CLORE: Content-Level Optimization for Reasoning Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22211)
*Yuyang Wu,Qiyao Xue,Guanxing Lu,Weichen Liu,Zihan Wang,Manling Li,Olexandr Isayev*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: CLORE 通过对正确 on-policy rollouts 进行内容级编辑（删除重复/无关推理），结合 DPO 与策略梯度训练，在数学推理任务上实现了更好的精度-效率权衡，为长度控制提供了内容级监督的补充方案。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 当前大语言模型的强化学习后训练虽提升了推理能力，但常产生不必要冗长、重复或语义模糊的推理痕迹。现有高效推理方法主要通过显式预算或长度感知奖励来调节响应长度，对中间推理内容的监督较弱。

Method: 提出 CLORE，一种内容级优化框架，通过外部增强模型删除正确 on-policy rollouts 中的重复片段、难辨内容、任务无关内容及解题后多余的推理过程，同时保留最终答案。利用辅助的参考模型无关的 DPO 目标函数与标准策略梯度训练联合优化，限制增强操作仅在正确轨迹上进行并执行局部删除，保持编辑后的 rollout 接近策略分布。

Result: 在 DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B 和 Qwen2.5-Math-7B 上跨五个数学推理基准的实验表明，CLORE 改善了精度-效率权衡，并与 GRPO、DAPO、Training Efficient 及 ThinkPrune 方法兼容。

Conclusion: CLORE通过在正确轨迹上进行局部删除，保持了编辑后 rollout 与策略分布的接近性，有效缓解了 off-policy 不匹配问题。内容级分析表明该方法减少了重复推理、难辨内容和答案后探索，验证了内容级监督作为长度级控制补充方向的可行性。

Abstract: Reinforcement learning post-training has improved the reasoning ability of large language models, but often produces unnecessarily long, repetitive, or semantically opaque reasoning traces. Existing efficient reasoning methods mainly regulate response length through explicit budgets or length-aware rewards, leaving intermediate reasoning content weakly supervised. We propose CLORE, a content-level optimization framework that improves reasoning efficiency by editing correct on-policy rollouts. CLORE uses an external augmentation model to delete repetitive segments, illegible or task-irrelevant content, and superfluous reasoning after the solution is established, while preserving the final answer. The resulting augmented--original pairs are optimized with an auxiliary reference-free DPO objective alongside standard policy-gradient training. By restricting augmentation to correct trajectories and performing local deletion, CLORE keeps edited rollouts close to the policy distribution and mitigates off-policy mismatch. Experiments on DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B and Qwen2.5-Math-7B across five mathematical reasoning benchmarks show that CLORE improves the accuracy--efficiency trade-off and remains compatible with GRPO, DAPO, Training Efficient, and ThinkPrune. Content-level analyses further show that CLORE reduces repetitive reasoning, illegible content, and post-answer exploration, supporting content-level supervision as a complementary direction to length-level control.

</details>


### [261] [Towards a compositional semantics for quantitative confidence assessment in assurance arguments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22213)
*Benjamin Herd,Jessica Kelly,Jan Sabsch,Lydia Gauerhof*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 本文提出了一种基于主观逻辑（SL）的置信度语义框架，将保证论证中的元素表示为SL观点，将元素间关系映射为SL算子，实现论证置信度的系统化传播与分析。该方法兼容GSNNotation，提供明确的保证、上下文处理和溯源能力。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing assurance argument notations (e.g., GSN) lack operational semantics for deriving assurance confidence, and while Subjective Logic offers belief calculi, existing SL-based approaches do not provide a uniform, compositional semantics covering all argument elements and relations.

Method: Map argument elements to SL opinions and model relations between elements using SL operators to create an analyzable confidence network.

Result: The approach effectively turns assurance arguments into analyzable confidence networks with principled confidence propagation under incomplete, conflicting, or subjective evidence.

Conclusion: The proposed approach provides explicit warrants, principled handling of context, preserved provenance, and compatibility with GSN, enabling overall confidence assessment of assurance arguments through a uniform, compositional semantics.

Abstract: Assurance arguments provide a clear and structured way to explain why stakeholders should trust that a system satisfies certain properties, yet widely used notations, e.g.Goal Structuring Notation (GSN), typically lack an operational semantics for deriving assurance confidence. Existing approaches address structure and soundness but largely reason over truth values, not over confidence in the justification of claims. Subjective Logic (SL) offers a calculus of belief, disbelief, and uncertainty with operators for combining opinions, enabling confidence propagation under incomplete, conflicting, or subjective evidence. However, existing SL-based approaches do not provide a uniform, compositional semantics that covers all argument elements and relations to enable overall confidence assessment. We propose a confidence semantics that represents argument elements as SL opinions and maps relations between elements to SL operators modelling how confidence flows, effectively turning the argument into an analyzable confidence network. The approach provides explicit warrants, principled handling of context, preserved provenance, and compatibility with GSN, along with practical guidance using an exemplary assurance confidence assessment.

</details>


### [262] [Evaluating Large Language Models as Live Strategic Agents: Provider Performance, Hybrid Decomposition, and Operational Gaps in Timed Risk Play](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22238)
*H. C. Ekne*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: A 32-game Risk championship comparing leading LLMs as live agents reveals that gemini-3.1-pro-preview significantly outperforms competitors, but this advantage largely disappears when planning is isolated from execution—suggesting real-world agent performance depends on objective tracking and execution conversion beyond pure reasoning ability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Static benchmarks cannot capture real-world LLM behavior in dynamic, repeated-loop settings with time limits, formatting constraints, and failure modes.

Method: Timed multi-phase Risk environment with victory targets; 32-game cross-provider championship under frozen rules; standardized execution on Gemini Flash scaffold to separate planning from execution; trace analysis of planning and execution behaviors.

Result: gemini-3.1-pro-preview won 20/32 games (p ≈ 1.5×10⁻⁵); however, when execution was standardized, planner performance showed near-equality (p ≈ 0.821), indicating provider spread came from end-to-end system behavior. Gemini exhibited stronger objective tracking and more deep conquest chains.

Conclusion: Live-agent performance depends on objective tracking, execution conversion, cost, and runtime reliability. Evaluating LLMs as components in bounded workflows is recommended instead of isolated benchmark respondents.

Abstract: Static benchmarks capture only part of how large language models behave in practice. Real systems place models inside repeated loops with time limits, formatting constraints, and failure modes. We study this setting in a timed multi-phase Risk environment with explicit victory targets and repeated planning and execution cycles. In a replicated 32-game cross-provider championship under frozen rules, gemini-3.1-pro-preview won 20 of 32 games against gpt-5.1, claude-opus-4-7, and kimi-k2.6, and the pooled winner distribution differs strongly from an equal-strength null (p approx 1.5 x 10^-5). We then separate planning from execution by standardizing execution on a cheaper Gemini Flash scaffold. Under this design, a pooled 32-game planner bakeoff is consistent with near-equality (p approx 0.821), which indicates that much of the earlier provider spread came from end-to-end system behavior rather than planning alone. To study mechanism, we analyze saved planning and execution traces from the provider championship. Gemini refers to the terminal objective far more often than the other models and increases that focus as victory approaches. Gemini also converts more turns into deep conquest chains, even though it is not the cleanest runtime. These results show that live-agent performance depends on objective tracking, execution conversion, cost, and runtime reliability, and they support evaluating LLMs as components in bounded workflows rather than as isolated benchmark respondents.

</details>


### [263] [Unlocking Proactivity in Task-Oriented Dialogue](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22240)
*Hongbin Zhang,Ning Gao,Yuqin Dai,Ruiyuan Wu,Jinpeng Wang,Rena Wei Gao,Bingdong Tan,Shuzheng Gao,Zongjie Li,Chaozheng Wang*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 本文针对后训练 LLM 在主动任务导向对话中表现保守的问题，提出用户潜在顾虑是关键训练信号。通过构建 Cognitive User Simulator 模拟用户分层特征，并引入 Simulator-Induced Asymmetric-View Policy Optimization 方法（包含 ASDP 和 STPR 两个目标），实现担忧感知行为的跨视角迁移和状态转移优化，从而解锁 LLM 的主动说服能力。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 主动任务导向对话（如主动销售）需要具有说服力的智能体，但后训练的 LLM 本质上保守；基于奖励塑形的 RL（如 GRPO）仅重新加权已被动策略采样的内容，难以激发主动能力。

Method: 构建 Cognitive User Simulator（认知用户模拟器）：将用户建模为分层 persona，包含可观察外部特征和隐藏内部顾虑；模拟器生成忠实且多样的交互，同时输出跟踪说服进度的每轮状态动态。引入 Simulator-Induced Asymmetric-View Policy Optimization 方法，包含两个训练目标：(1) Asymmetric On-Policy Self-Distillation (ASDP)：将同一策略的特权视角（可访问用户顾虑）的担忧感知行为迁移到可部署的仅对话视角；(2) State-Transition Policy Refinement (STPR)：利用模拟器状态转移优化策略。

Result: 用户潜在顾虑作为关键训练信号，可解锁主动能力；Cognitive User Simulator 可产生忠实且多样的交互并跟踪说服进度；ASDP 实现跨视角行为迁移，STPR 实现状态转移策略优化。

Conclusion: 通过用户潜在顾虑的条件化实现主动 persuasion；在模拟器上进行训练，避免采样破坏策略；ASDP 和 STPR 两个互补目标实现担忧感知行为的跨视角迁移和状态转移策略优化。

Abstract: Proactive task-oriented dialogue (TOD), such as outbound sales, demands a persuasive agent that actively probes the user's concerns and steers the conversation toward acceptance within a bounded number of turns. Yet post-trained LLMs are inherently conservative, and reward-shaping RL (e.g., GRPO) struggles since it only re-weights what an already passive policy samples. We show that conditioning on the user's latent concerns unlocks proactive capability that no amount of sampling can undermine, establishing these concerns as a pivotal training-time signal. To operationalize this finding, we build the \textbf{Cognitive User Simulator}, which models each user as a stratified persona comprising observable external traits and hidden internal concerns. The simulator produces faithful and diverse interactions, while emitting per-turn state dynamics that track persuasion progress. We then introduce \textbf{Simulator-Induced Asymmetric-View Policy Optimization}, which converts the modeled concerns and the simulation state transition into complementary training objectives: (1) \emph{Asymmetric On-Policy Self-Distillation} that transfers concern-aware behavior from a privileged view of the same policy into its deployable, conversation-only view; and (2) \emph{State-Transition Policy Refinement} ...

</details>


### [264] [SciCore-Mol: Augmenting Large Language Models with Pluggable Molecular Cognition Modules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22287)
*Yuxuan Chen,Changwei Lv,Yunduo Xiao,Zhongjing Du,Daquan Zhou,Yukun Yan,Zheni Zeng,Zhiyuan Liu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: SciCore-Mol是一个模块化框架，通过三个可插拔认知模块（拓扑感知、分子生成、反应推理）弥合LLM与分子科学数据之间的语义鸿沟，使8B开源模型在多项化学任务中达到与专有模型相当甚至更优的水平。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 大型语言模型(LLM)在处理异构科学数据（如分子）时面临根本性挑战：离散语言符号与拓扑分子数据或连续反应数据之间存在固有鸿沟，导致基于文本的推理过程中信息损失严重、语义噪声显著。

Method: SciCore-Mol框架通过三个深度集成的可插拔认知模块解决LLM处理科学数据的挑战：拓扑感知模块、基于潜在扩散的分子生成模块、以及反应感知推理模块。各模块通过学习到的表示接口与LLM主干耦合，实现比纯文本工具反馈更丰富的信息交换。

Result: SciCore-Mol在分子理解、生成、反式预测和通用化学知识的综合任务上展现出优异性能。8B参数开源系统在与专有大型模型的比较中具有竞争力，并在多个维度实现超越。

Conclusion: SciCore-Mol provides a systematic blueprint for equipping LLMs with scientific expertise through decoupled, pluggable, and flexibly orchestrated modules, with implications for drug design, chemical synthesis, and broader scientific discovery.

Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) are central to the one-for-all intelligent paradigm, but they face a fundamental challenge when dealing with heterogeneous scientific data such as molecules: the inherent gap between discrete linguistic symbols and topological molecular or continuous reaction data leads to significant information loss and semantic noise in text-based reasoning. We propose SciCore-Mol, a modular framework that bridges this gap through three deeply integrated pluggable cognitive modules: a topology-aware perception module, a latent diffusion-based molecular generation module, and a reaction-aware reasoning module. Each module is coupled to the LLM backbone through learned representation interfaces, enabling richer information exchange than is possible with text-only tool feedback. Our experiments on diverse chemical tasks demonstrate that SciCore-Mol achieves strong comprehensive performance across molecular understanding, generation, reaction prediction, and general chemistry knowledge, with an 8B-parameter open-source system that is competitive with and in several dimensions surpasses proprietary large models. This work provides a systematic blueprint for equipping LLMs with scientific expertise through decoupled, pluggable, and flexibly orchestrated modules, with direct implications for drug design, chemical synthesis, and broader scientific discovery.

</details>


### [265] [Meta-Soft: Leveraging Composable Meta-Tokens for Context-Preserving KV Cache Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22337)
*Wei Luo,Yi Huang,Songchen Ma,Huanyu Qu,Jiang Cai,Mingkun Xu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: The KV cache used in large language models has linearly growing time complexity, so LLMs face memory blow-up and reduced decoding efficiency when they process long contexts.Current KV Cache eviction has become an important research direction; however, existing methods based on fixed Soft Tokens (e.g., Judge Q) rely on a static parameter set as the query to evaluate the importance of KV pairs, so they cannot adapt dynamically to different input prompts, and they cannot precisely capture complex and changing task relevance.Also, evicted KV pairs are discarded permanently, so this causes irreversible information loss and context breaks. To address this problem, we propose Meta-Soft, a dynamic compression framework based on probe-driven context integration. Specifically, we build a meta-library with a learnable orthogonal basis matrix $\mathcal{L}$, and we use a selector network with Gumbel-Softmax to produce differentiable sparse combination weights, so we dynamically synthesize the most targeted $k$ Soft Tokens from the input prompt features.We append these Soft Tokens to the end of the input sequence to probe key information. We also introduce an attention-flow based integration mechanism, which redistributes the semantic information of removed tokens into retained tokens, and this keeps the dropped context information effectively.Experiments on multiple datasets show that our method outperforms existing state-of-the-art eviction methods and provides a new solution for KV Cache compression.

</details>


### [266] [S2ED: From Story to Executable Descriptions for Consistency-Aware Story Illustration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22448)
*Sijing Yin,Jiamou Liu,Xiao Tang,Yaser Shakib,Qian Liu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: S2ED: A training-free framework converting stories into executable descriptions for consistent multi-frame story illustration. Uses three coordinated agents for narrative segmentation, character grounding, and spatial/affective enrichment. Achieves superior consistency and character fidelity without retraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multi-frame story illustration requires long-horizon coherence beyond single-image generation, including narrative decomposition, persistent character identity, layout consistency, and affect preservation across frames. Existing text-to-image models struggle with these multi-frame consistency challenges.

Method: S2ED is a training-free, model-agnostic prompt-layer framework that coordinates three agents to: (1) segment narrative into frames, (2) ground canonical character attributes, and (3) enrich spatial and affective cues. It enables interpretable prompt-carried state propagation and allows local edits to repair drift without retraining the generator.

Result: Experiments on Flintstones and Shakoo Maku datasets show S2ED improves sequence-level consistency and character fidelity over strong prompting, large-model planning, and reference training-based methods, validated by both automatic metrics and human judgments.

Conclusion: S2ED provides an effective training-free, model-agnostic solution for multi-frame story illustration. The framework significantly improves sequence-level consistency and character fidelity compared to strong prompting, large-model planning, and reference training-based methods. Successfully deployed in an end-to-end story-to-storybook system for children's illustrated stories.

Abstract: Multi-frame story illustration requires long-horizon coherence beyond single-image text-to-image generation, including narrative decomposition and persistent character identity, layout, and affect across frames. We propose Story-to-Executable Descriptions (S2ED), a training-free, model-agnostic, prompt-layer framework that converts a full story into a sequence of explicit, editable executable descriptions for more consistent rendering. S2ED coordinates three agents to segment the narrative, ground canonical character attributes, and enrich spatial and affective cues, enabling interpretable prompt-carried state propagation and local edits to repair drift without retraining the generator. Experiments on Flintstones and Shakoo Maku show that S2ED improves sequence-level consistency and character fidelity over strong prompting, large-model planning, and a reference training-based method, under both automatic metrics and human judgments. We also deploy S2ED in an end-to-end story-to-storybook system for children's illustrated stories, with a supplementary video.

</details>


### [267] [KAPPS: A knowledge-based CPPS Architecture for the Circular Factory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22457)
*Etienne Hoffmann,Jan-Felix Klein,Sören Weindel,Max Goebels,Sebastian Behrendt,Daniel Hernández,Ratan Bahadur Thapa,Jürgen Fleischer,Kai Furmans,Steffen Staab*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 针对循环制造中产品状态异构、不确定的特点，提出传统制造IT架构难以胜任的问题，论文采用设计科学研究方法，从五个视角推导出14项需求，设计了基于本体的知识图谱架构KAPPS作为统一数据骨干，配合语义接口层实现异构系统集成与推理，并具备约束执行与事件驱动规划模块，通过两个用例验证了架构的适用性。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Circular manufacturing introduces used products with heterogeneous/uncertain conditions requiring systems that handle variable product states, dynamically reconfigurable processes, and human-machine knowledge integration—capabilities that conventional IT architectures (designed for stable structures and deterministic execution) cannot provide.

Method: Design science methodology to develop a Cyber Physical Production System for circular manufacturing; requirements derived from five complementary perspectives; architecture design centered on ontology-grounded knowledge graph as unifying data backbone with semantic interface layer.

Result: KAPPS architecture: knowledge graph as authoritative write-time state enabling consistent data/information integration, reasoning, and cross-system communication; modules for constraint enforcement and event-driven planning supporting incremental execution plan adaptation under uncertainty and human-machine knowledge exchange.

Conclusion: KAPPS demonstrated via two use cases (anomaly detection via knowledge graph services; runtime constraint enforcement in modular conveyor system) and evaluated against all 14 requirements.

Abstract: While linear manufacturing relies on homogeneous materials and predefined process sequences, circular manufacturing reintroduces used products with heterogeneous and uncertain conditions. This shift demands manufacturing systems capable of handling variable product states, dynamically reconfigurable processes, and the integration of human and machine knowledge. Conventional manufacturing IT architectures, designed for stable structures and deterministic execution, are unable to meet these requirements, as they cannot adequately represent and manage the uniqueness of individual components at runtime. Following a design science methodology for developing a Cyber Physical Production System for circular manufacturing, we derive 14 requirements from five complementary perspectives. Based on these requirements, we design KAPPS, a knowledge-based architecture that uses an ontology-grounded knowledge graph as a unifying data backbone, combined with a semantic interface layer to enable consistent data and information integration, reasoning, and communication across heterogeneous systems and services, turning the knowledge graph from an integration layer into the factories authoritative write-time state. KAPPS incorporates modules for constraint enforcement and event-driven planning, enabling incremental adaptation of execution plans under uncertainty and human-machine knowledge exchange. The applicability of KAPPS is demonstrated through two implemented use cases: (i) Anomaly detection and learning through knowledge graph mediated services and (ii) runtime constraint enforcement in a modular conveyor system. Subsequently, the architecture is evaluated against the 14 requirements (ed. abstract shortened)

</details>


### [268] [Compiling Agentic Workflows into LLM Weights: Near-Frontier Quality at Two Orders of Magnitude Less Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22502)
*Simon Dennis,Rivaan Patil,Kevin Shabahang,Hao Guo*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: This paper investigates why developers prefer external orchestration frameworks over fine-tuned "subterranean agents" for procedural tasks, identifies three perceived barriers, and empirically demonstrates that fine-tuned small models can match orchestration performance while eliminating context window overhead, frontier model dependency, and proprietary procedure exposure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Despite evidence that compiling procedures into fine-tuned small models works for procedural tasks, developer adoption has overwhelmingly favored orchestration frameworks, raising the question of why this adoption gap exists.

Method: Comparative evaluation across three complex procedural domains (travel booking, Zoom support, insurance claims) to empirically address three perceived barriers against fine-tuned approaches.

Result: Fine-tuned models achieve comparable performance to orchestration approaches on complex multi-step procedures across different scales (14-55 nodes).

Conclusion: Fine-tuned subterranean agents can match or exceed orchestration frameworks for procedural tasks while addressing context window consumption, frontier model dependency, and proprietary procedure exposure.

Abstract: Agent orchestration frameworks have proliferated, collectively exceeding 290,000 GitHub stars across LangGraph, CrewAI, Google ADK, OpenAI Agents SDK, Semantic Kernel, Strands, and LlamaIndex. All follow the same pattern: an external orchestrator above the LLM, injecting instructions and routing decisions every turn. Recent work has shown this architecture is dominated for procedural tasks by simply providing the procedure in a frontier model's system prompt [Dennis et al., 2026a], at the cost of consuming the context window, requiring a frontier model for every conversation, and exposing proprietary procedures to third-party providers. Compiling the procedure into the weights of a small fine-tuned model -- creating a subterranean agent -- should resolve all of these concerns, and prior work (SimpleTOD, FireAct, SynTOD, WorkflowLLM, Agent Lumos) has shown the technique works. Yet developer adoption has overwhelmingly favored orchestration. We identify three perceived barriers and address each empirically across travel booking (14 nodes), Zoom support (14 nodes, product-specific knowledge), and insurance claims (55 nodes, 6 decision hubs).

</details>


### [269] [LACO: Adaptive Latent Communication for Collaborative Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22504)
*Tianhao Chen,Yuheng Wu,Dongman Lee*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: LACO proposes a training-free latent communication paradigm for collaborative driving, using Iterative Latent Deliberation, Cross-Horizon Saliency Attribution, and Structured Semantic Knowledge Distillation to reduce latency and information loss while maintaining collaborative performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Language-based communication in collaborative driving introduces two key challenges: high latency from autoregressive decoding and information loss from compressing internal representations into discrete tokens.

Method: Three technical components: Iterative Latent Deliberation (ILD) for latent reasoning, Cross-Horizon Saliency Attribution (CHSA) for communication-efficient information selection, and Structured Semantic Knowledge Distillation (SSKD) to stabilize ego-centric decision making.

Result: The proposed training-free LACO paradigm effectively addresses latent communication challenges while notably reducing communication and inference latency while maintaining strong collaborative driving performance.

Conclusion: LACO notably reduces communication and inference latency while maintaining strong collaborative driving performance, validated through closed-loop experiments in CARLA.

Abstract: Collaborative driving aims to improve safety and efficiency by enabling connected vehicles to coordinate under partial observability. Recent approaches have evolved from sharing visual features for perception to exchanging language-based reasoning through foundation models for behavioral coordination. Though communicating in language provides intuitive information, it introduces two challenges: high latency caused by autoregressive decoding and information loss caused by compressing rich internal representations into discrete tokens. To address these challenges, we analyze latent communication in collaborative driving under inherent limitations of multi-agent settings. Our analysis reveals agent identity confusion, where direct fusion of latent states entangles decision representations across vehicles. Motivated by this, we propose LACO, a training-free \textbf{LA}tent \textbf{CO}mmunication paradigm that seamlessly adapts pretrained driving models to collaborative settings. LACO introduces Iterative Latent Deliberation (ILD) for latent reasoning, Cross-Horizon Saliency Attribution (CHSA) for communication-efficient information selection, and Structured Semantic Knowledge Distillation (SSKD) to stabilize ego-centric decision making. Closed-loop experiments in CARLA show that LACO notably reduces communication and inference latency while maintaining strong collaborative driving performance.

</details>


### [270] [Towards Direct Evaluation of Harness Optimizers via Priority Ranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22505)
*Kai Tzu-iunn Ong,Minseok Kang,Dongwook Choi,Junhee Cho,Seungju Kim,Seungwon Lim,Geunha Jang,Minwoo Oh,Bogyung Jeong,Sunghwan Kim,Taeyoon Kwon,Jinyoung Yeo*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 本文针对harness优化器的直接评估问题，提出了一种低成本设计——priority ranking，通过让优化器对组件进行优先级排序来量化其优化能力，无需昂贵的rollout或人工检查；在包含182个场景的Shor数据集上验证了该方法的有效性。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有研究仅通过观察目标agent的性能提升来评估优化器，这种间接的终态评估忽略了优化器在中间步骤的actions——这些actions往往充满错误且阻碍agent性能；因此无法判断harness优化是得益于优化器的明智更新还是简单的试错

Method: 通过让优化器对给定harness中的组件（如工具）按其对agent性能的潜在改善/阻碍程度排序，直接量化优化器在步骤级别的能力

Result: 优化器的排序表现与其在实际多步harness优化中提升agent的能力相关，验证了priority ranking可作为优化能力的可靠预测指标

Conclusion: priority ranking能可靠预测优化器能力；Shor数据集包含182个人类验证的优化场景

Abstract: Harness optimization enables automated agent creation by having an optimizer agent iteratively update the harness of target agents. Despite its success, current studies evaluate optimizers solely by observing target agents' performance gains. This indirect end-improvement evaluation neglects optimizers' actions at intermediate steps, which are often erroneous and hinder agent performance. Therefore, it is unclear whether harness optimization is driven by optimizers' informed update actions or simply trial-and-error. This necessitates direct evaluation of harness optimizers. However, evaluating harness optimizers directly is non-trivial and costly due to the lack of oracle harnesses. To address this, we present a simple, low-cost design to directly evaluate them, namely priority ranking. By asking harness optimizers to rank components (e.g., tools) in a given harness by their potential to improve/hinder agent performance when updated, our design quantifies optimizer ability at the step level without expensive rollouts or manual examination. More importantly, optimizers' ranking performance correlates with their ability to improve agents in actual multi-step harness optimization, establishing priority ranking as a reliable predictor of optimization ability. Priority ranking is enabled by Shor, a collection of 182 human-verified optimization scenarios spanning across domains, designs, and time stages. Codes and data can be found at https://github.com/k59118/Harness_Optimizer_Evaluation.

</details>


### [271] [Meta-Learning for Rapid Adaptation in Reference Tracking of Uncertain Nonlinear Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22513)
*Jiaqi Yan,Ankush Chakrabarty,Niklas Schmid,John Lygeros,Alisa Rupenyan*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: A meta-learning-based control framework is proposed for reference tracking of uncertain nonlinear systems with limited data, combining offline meta-training and online meta-adaptation phases via bi-level optimization, with flexible algorithm choices and demonstrated superior performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Collecting data from target systems is challenging; thus, optimal controllers must be designed with limited target system data. Meta-learning offers a solution by leveraging offline data from structurally similar source systems.

Method: The paper proposes a bi-level optimization framework that adapts iMAML to control settings, operating in two phases: (1) Offline meta-training to learn aggregated representations from source systems; (2) Online meta-adaptation using few samples and limited steps on target system. Two instantiations are provided using neural state-space model and deep Q-network respectively.

Result: The methods enhance control performance and consistently outperform baseline approaches in both numerical simulations and hardware experiments.

Conclusion: The proposed meta-learning control framework provides a general and flexible approach for reference tracking, with demonstrated effectiveness through both numerical simulations and hardware experiments.

Abstract: In this paper, we address the problem of reference tracking for uncertain nonlinear systems. Since collecting data from the target system (i.e., the system of interest) is often challenging, our objective is to design optimal controllers using limited target system data. Meta-learning provides a promising paradigm by leveraging offline data from source systems (systems sharing structural similarities with the target system) to accelerate training and enhance control performance. Motivated by this idea, we propose a meta-learning-based control framework that tailors the implicit model-agnostic meta-learning (iMAML) algorithm to the control setting. The framework operates in two phases: an (offline) meta-training phase, where an aggregated representation is learned from source data to capture the shared system dynamics among similar systems, and an (online) meta-adaptation phase, where this representation is fine-tuned on the target system using only a few data samples and limited adaptation steps. We formulate this framework as a bi-level optimization problem and provide an efficient solution with reduced storage complexity and few approximations. The proposed framework is general, allowing various learning algorithms to be integrated. To demonstrate this flexibility, we propose two specific learning algorithms that can be incorporated into our framework based on a neural state-space model and a deep Q-network, respectively. The primary distinction between these approaches is whether explicit system identification is required. Numerical simulations and hardware experiments demonstrate that the proposed methods enhance control performance and consistently outperform baseline approaches.

</details>


### [272] [A Subjective Logic-based method for runtime confidence updates in safety arguments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22530)
*Benjamin Herd,Jessica Kelly,Clarissa Heinemann,João-Vitor Zacchi*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: A dynamic quantitative assurance method combining static safety cases with runtime SPI evidence using Subjective Logic, featuring a safety-prioritized confidence update rule (rather than exact Bayesian updates), demonstrated on an ML-based construction cone detection system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Static safety cases alone cannot capture runtime operational evidence, creating a gap between design-time assurance and actual operational performance—particularly critical for ML-based components with inherent uncertainty.

Method: Subjective Logic (SL)-based assurance case integrating design-time evidence with windowed runtime Safety Performance Indicators (SPIs); rule-based confidence update mechanism prioritizing safety responsiveness over exact Bayesian posterior computation.

Result: The method enables continuous confidence evolution as SPI evidence is observed; the rule-based approach provides faster safety-relevant responsiveness compared to traditional Bayesian updates while maintaining principled uncertainty quantification.

Conclusion: The paper demonstrates feasibility of unified design-time/runtime assurance through a simulation case study, with confidence evolving meaningfully as SPI evidence accumulates during operation.

Abstract: We present a method for dynamic quantitative assurance that enhances static safety cases with continuous, runtime-driven confidence updates. The method quantifies and propagates confidence across the development lifecycle by integrating design-time evidence and windowed runtime Safety Performance Indicators (SPIs) within a single Subjective Logic (SL)-based assurance case. At runtime, SPI evidence is continuously evaluated, and targeted claims are updated using a rule that increases confidence in the absence of violations and imposes prompt penalties when violations occur. This design prioritizes safety-relevant responsiveness over exact classical Bayesian posterior updates. We demonstrate the method using a simulation-based construction zone assist function, focusing on an ML-based construction cone detection component, and show how confidence evolves as SPI evidence is observed in operation.

</details>


### [273] [Think Thrice Before You Speak: Dual knowledge-enhanced Theory-of-Mind Reasoning for Persuasive Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22602)
*Minghui Ma,Bin Guo,Runze Yang,Mengqi Chen,Yan Liu,Jingqi Liu,Yahan Pei,Xuehao Ma,Qiuyun Zhang,Zhiwen Yu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 本文针对说服对话中的心智理论（ToM）推理挑战，提出ToM-PD任务和ToM-BPD大规模标注数据集，并设计TTBYS逐步推理框架。实验证明Qwen3-8B配合该框架在多项预测任务上显著超越GPT-5，特别是在信念预测方面提升达22.80%。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有LLM由于依赖简单提示策略和缺乏足够的ToM知识，往往无法捕捉心理状态间的内在依赖关系，导致表示碎片化和推理不稳定。这限制了其在需要推理他人心理状态的说服对话中的表现。

Method: 提出TTBYS（Think Thrice Before You Speak）框架，利用显式和隐式先验经验，通过逐步推理方式增强LLM对欲望、信念和说服策略的推断能力。该框架基于BDI模型建模心理状态间的序列依赖关系。

Result: 装备TTBYS的Qwen3-8B在欲望预测、信念预测和说服策略预测三项任务上分别超越GPT-5达1.20%、22.80%和16.97%。案例研究表明该方法增强了推理的可解释性和一致性。

Conclusion: 该研究提出了ToM-PD任务、ToM-BPD数据集和TTBYS框架，有效解决了LLM在说服对话中因缺乏ToM能力而导致的表示碎片化和推理不稳定问题。实验表明该方法在各项指标上均显著优于GPT-5，尤其在信念预测上提升达22.80%，且增强了推理的可解释性和一致性。

Abstract: Persuasive dialogue requires reasoning about others' latent mental states, a capability known as Theory of Mind (ToM). However, due to reliance on simple prompting strategies and insufficient ToM knowledge, existing LLMs often fail to capture the intrinsic dependencies among mental states, leading to fragmented representations and unstable reasoning. To address these challenges, we introduce the ToM-based Persuasive Dialogue (ToM-PD) task, grounded in the Belief-Desire-Intention (BDI) framework, which explicitly models the sequential dependencies among mental states in multi-turn dialogues. To facilitate research on this task, we construct a large-scale annotated dataset, ToM-based Broad Persuasive Dialogues (ToM-BPD), capturing fine-grained mental states and corresponding persuasive strategies. We further propose Think Thrice Before You Speak (TTBYS), a knowledge-enhanced stepwise reasoning framework that leverages both explicit and implicit prior experiences to improve LLMs' inference of desires, beliefs, and persuasive strategies. Experimental results demonstrate that Qwen3-8B equipped with TTBYS outperforms GPT-5 by 1.20%, 22.80%, and 16.97% in predicting desires, beliefs, and persuasive strategies, respectively. Case studies further show that our approach enhances interpretability and consistency in reasoning.

</details>


### [274] [Spreadsheet-RL: Advancing Large Language Model Agents on Realistic Spreadsheet Tasks via Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22642)
*Banghao Chi,Yining Xie,Mingyuan Wu,Jingcheng Yang,Jize Jiang,Zhaoheng Li,Shengyi Qian,Minjia Zhang,Klara Nahrstedt,Rui Hou,Xiangjun Fan,Hanchao Yu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 本文提出Spreadsheet-RL，一个用于训练专业电子表格智能体的强化学习微调框架，通过自动化数据收集、领域特定基准数据集和专用训练环境，显著提升了LLM在电子表格任务上的表现。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有电子表格智能体依赖通用LLM的专用提示方法，在简单操作上有效，但在处理现实世界复杂多步骤工作流时表现不佳。

Method: Spreadsheet-RL是一个强化学习微调框架，包含三个核心组件：1）自动化数据收集管道，从在线论坛收集成对的起始-目标电子表格；2）Domain-Spreadsheet基准数据集，包含金融和供应链等领域的评估任务；3）Spreadsheet Gym环境，通过Python沙箱暴露Excel功能，并提供综合工具集和工具路由规则，支持多轮强化学习训练。

Result: Spreadsheet-RL显著提升了性能：Qwen3-4B-Thinking-2507在SpreadsheetBench上的Pass@1从12.0%提升至23.4%，在Domain-Spreadsheet数据集上从8.4%提升至17.2%。

Conclusion: Spreadsheet-RL显著提升了AI智能体在电子表格任务上的表现，展示了良好的泛化能力和实际应用潜力，为基于LLM的数据界面交互研究提供了新范式。

Abstract: Spreadsheet systems (e.g., Microsoft Excel, Google Sheets) play a central role in modern data-centric workflows. As AI agents grow increasingly capable of automating complex tasks, such as controlling computers and generating presentations, building an AI-driven spreadsheet agent has emerged as a promising research direction. Most existing spreadsheet agents rely on specialized prompting over general-purpose LLMs; while this design has potentials on simple spreadsheet operations, it struggles to manage the complex, multi-step workflows typical of real-world applications.
  We introduce Spreadsheet-RL, a reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning framework designed to train specialized spreadsheet agents within a realistic Microsoft Excel environment. Spreadsheet-RL features an automated pipeline for scalable collection of paired start-goal spreadsheets from online forums, as well as domain-specific evaluation tasks in areas such as finance and supply chain management, which we compile into the new Domain-Spreadsheet benchmark dataset. It also includes a Spreadsheet Gym environment designed for multi-turn RL: Spreadsheet Gym exposes extensive Excel functionality through a Python sandbox, along with a refined harness that incorporates a comprehensive tool set and carefully designed tool-routing rules for spreadsheet tasks. Through comprehensive experiments, we show that Spreadsheet-RL substantially enhances AI agent's performance on both general and domain-specific spreadsheet tasks: it improves Qwen3-4B-Thinking-2507's Pass@1 on SpreadsheetBench from 12.0% to 23.4%, and raises Pass@1 from 8.4% to 17.2% on our curated Domain-Spreadsheet dataset. These results highlight Spreadsheet-RL's strong potential for generalization and real-world adoption in spreadsheet automation, and broadly, its promise for advancing LLM-based interactions with data interfaces in everyday work.

</details>


### [275] [Claw AI Lab: An Autonomous Multi-Agent Research Team](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22662)
*Fan Wu,Cheng Chen,Zhenshan Tan,Taiyu Zhang,Xinzhen Xu,Yanyu Qian,Dingcheng Gao,Lanyun Zhu,Qi Zhu,Yi Tan,Deyi Ji,Guosheng Lin,Tianrun Chen,Deheng Ye,Fayao Liu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Claw AI Lab transforms autonomous research from static pipelines into interactive AI laboratories by enabling customizable multi-agent research teams, comprehensive workflow monitoring/control, and a Claw-Code Harness for robust execution integration, improving both research quality and reliability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current automated research systems operate as hidden prompt-to-paper pipelines with limited interactivity, inspectability, and reliability. Existing approaches lack customizable multi-agent teams, comprehensive workflow control, and robust execution integration with local development environments.

Method: Claw AI Lab instantiates customizable research teams from prompts with collaborative workflows, real-time monitoring, artifact inspection, and rollback/resume control via a unified dashboard. The Claw-Code Harness connects local codebases, datasets, and checkpoints to runnable experiments. Supports exploration, multi-agent discussion, and reproduction modes.

Result: In internal evaluation on 5 AI research case studies, Claw AI Lab was consistently preferred by AI expert judges on idea novelty, experiment completeness, and paper presentation quality compared to AutoResearchClaw baseline.

Conclusion: Claw AI Lab represents a paradigm shift toward autonomous research as usable, interactive, and reliability-aware scientific infrastructure. It improves execution integration, experimental completion, and result integrity, while being preferred by AI experts on idea novelty, experiment completeness, and paper presentation quality.

Abstract: We present Claw AI Lab, a lab-native autonomous research platform that advances automated research from a hidden prompt-to-paper pipeline into an interactive AI laboratory. Rather than centering the system around a single agent or a fixed serial workflow, we allow users to instantiate a full research team from one prompt, with customizable roles, collaborative workflows, real-time monitoring, artifact inspection, and rollback/resume control through a unified dashboard. The platform also supports distinct research modes for exploration, multi-agent discussion, and reproduction, making autonomous research substantially more steerable and laboratory-like in practice. A key practical contribution of Claw AI Lab lies in its Claw-Code Harness, which connects local codebases, datasets, and checkpoints to runnable experiments and feeds execution artifacts back into the research loop. As a result, the harness improves not only execution integration, but also experimental completion and result integrity: experiments are easier to inspect, iterate on, and faithfully transfer into final papers, reducing common failure modes such as partial runs and malformed result reporting. In our internal evaluation on five AI research case studies, using AutoResearchClaw as the baseline, Claw AI Lab is consistently preferred by AI expert judges on idea novelty, experiment completeness, and paper presentation quality. We view Claw AI Lab as an early step toward a new paradigm: autonomous research as usable, interactive, and reliability-aware scientific infrastructure.

</details>


### [276] [Is Capability a Liability? More Capable Language Models Make Worse Forecasts When It Matters Most](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22672)
*Nick Merrill,Jaeho Lee,Ezra Karger*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: LLMs exhibit inverse scaling on forecasting tasks involving superlinear growth and tail risk regimes (common in finance and epidemiology). More capable models produce worse distributional forecasts by over-extrapolating in the upper tail. Standard single-threshold benchmarks mask this effect; tail-inclusive scoring reveals it.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing LLM benchmarks miss upper-tail forecast failures because they rely on single-threshold metrics that are insensitive to distributional errors on superlinear growth and tail-risk tasks.

Method: Developed FBSim (contamination-free benchmark), conducted per-quantile decomposition, within-family ablation (Llama-3.1), and compared single-threshold vs. tail-inclusive scoring.

Result: More capable models produce worse distributional forecasts by shifting the upper tail upward to track aggressive extrapolations, concentrating failures in the upper tail.

Conclusion: Inverse scaling on tail risks can be masked by single-threshold metrics; tail-inclusive scoring reveals the true capability-accuracy relationship, which is negative for superlinear growth tasks.

Abstract: We document inverse scaling in LLMs on forecasting problems whose underlying time series exhibit superlinear growth and tail risk of regime change, a structure common in finance and epidemiology. On these tasks, more capable models produce worse distributional forecasts. The pattern appears on ForecastBench-Sim (FBSim), a contamination-free, simulated-world benchmark we release, in forecasting synthetic SIR epidemics with a matched linear control, and replicates in real-world datasets on COVID-19, measles, housing markets, and hyperinflation. A per-quantile decomposition shows the failure concentrates at the upper tail, which more capable models shift upward to track aggressive extrapolations of growth, while the lower tail stays put. A within-family study of Llama-3.1 shows that both model scale and post-training independently contribute to this effect. Domain knowledge does not reliably rescue calibration. This inverse scaling does not appear on single-threshold metrics common in LLM forecasting benchmarks, reversing the sign of the capability--accuracy relationship on identical outputs. Single-threshold scoring at conventional cutoffs misses the upper-tail cost; tail-inclusive scoring reverses the sign of the capability--accuracy relationship on the same outputs. We recommend that LLM forecasting evaluations use continuous (and unbounded) measures of accuracy alongside bounded binary threshold metrics.

</details>


### [277] [Forecasting Scientific Progress with Artificial Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22681)
*Sean Wu,Pan Lu,Yupeng Chen,Jonathan Bragg,Yutaro Yamada,Peter Clark,David Clifton,Philip Torr,James Zou,Junchi Yu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 本文提出CUSP基准用于评估AI预测科学进步的能力，发现当前前沿模型在预测科学突破是否实现及发生时间上存在系统性失败，性能对训练截断不敏感，且prior knowledge不能转化为可靠预测，模型对高影响力进展的预测尤为困难。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 人工智能已深度嵌入科学发现过程，但其能否预测科学进步仍不清楚。现有研究缺乏在可控知识约束条件下对科学预测能力进行系统评估的框架。

Method: 研究团队提出CUSP基准测试，包含4,760个科学事件，涵盖多学科领域，从可行性评估、机制推理、生成性解决方案设计和时间预测四个维度进行评估。通过控制知识获取条件，系统性地探究模型在不同信息环境下的表现差异。

Result: 前沿模型在4,760个科学事件上表现出系统性和领域依赖性的局限性。具体发现：1）模型能够从竞争性候选方案中识别出合理研究方向，但无法可靠预测科学突破是否会被实现及何时实现；2）性能在不同领域差异显著，AI领域进展的可预测性高于生物、化学和物理领域；3）模型对训练截断点前后的事件性能不敏感，表明局限性不能单纯归因于训练数据中的知识暴露；4）在受控信息访问条件下，额外的截断前知识虽能提升性能，但无法弥合与全信息环境的差距，且对于高被引用的突破这一差距更为明显；5）模型表现出系统性的过度自信和强烈的响应偏差，不确定性估计不可靠。

Conclusion: 当前AI系统作为科学进步预测工具仍存在显著不足。prior knowledge的获取并不能转化为可靠的预测能力，模型在获得事后信息时的表现远优于前瞻性预测。

Abstract: Artificial intelligence (AI) is increasingly embedded in scientific discovery, yet whether it can anticipate scientific progress remains unclear. To study this question, we introduce a temporally grounded evaluation framework for forecasting scientific progress under controlled knowledge constraints. We present CUSP (Cutoff-conditioned Unseen Scientific Progress), a multi-disciplinary and event-level benchmark that evaluates scientific forecasting in AI systems through feasibility assessment, mechanistic reasoning, generative solution design, and temporal prediction. Across 4,760 scientific events, we observe systematic and domain-dependent limitations in current frontier models. While models can identify plausible research directions from competing candidates, they fail to reliably predict whether scientific advances will be realized and systematically misestimate when they will occur. Performance is highly heterogeneous across domains, with the timing of AI progress more predictable than advances in biology, chemistry, and physics. Performance is largely insensitive to whether events occur before or after the training cutoff, suggesting these limitations cannot be explained solely by knowledge exposure in training data. Under controlled information access, additional pre-cutoff knowledge improves performance but does not close the gap to full-information settings, which becomes more pronounced for high-citation advances. Models also exhibit systematic overconfidence and strong response biases, indicating unreliable uncertainty estimation. Taken together, current AI systems fall short as predictive tools for scientific progress. Access to prior knowledge does not translate into reliable forecasting, and performance benefits more from post-event information than from forward-looking prediction.

</details>


### [278] [Beyond the Org Chart: AI and the Transformation of Invisible Work](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22707)
*Stephanie Rosenthal,Shamsi Iqbal*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 本研究通过对大型科技公司24名产品人员的访谈，揭示了AI正在改变工作场所的正式职责和非正式文化实践（如职业指导），AI既带来协作效率提升，也使职业成长机会（如获取反馈、领导力培养）面临风险，研究呼吁AI公司和领导者采取行动使"隐形工作"可见并支持健康的公司文化。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: AI采用正在帮助专业人士模糊和扩展其企业角色边界，但尚不清楚工作流程如何实际变化。

Method: 对大型科技公司24名产品导向人员进行了访谈，探讨AI如何影响：1）个人工作；2）产品团队内的工作；3）职业互动。研究聚焦于正式和非正式层面的变化。

Result: AI不仅改变了正式角色职责和协作模式，还改变了非正式文化实践（如职业指导）。部分变化积极（如同伴协作更顺畅），但更多变化具有风险（如职业成长机会减少、反馈和领导力培养机会受损）。

Conclusion: 提出了具体建议：AI公司应使"隐形工作"可见，个人和领导者应支持同事度过AI转型期，同时保留支持多元思维、协作和正式互动。AI的影响并非单一正面或负面，而是需要设计性干预以平衡效率提升与文化保护之间的张力。

Abstract: An increasing number of news and research articles report that AI adoption is allowing professionals to blur and extend the boundaries of their corporate roles. With the goal of understanding how work processes might be changing in an AI-forward company, we interviewed 24 product-focused individuals at a large technology firm about how AI has impacted their own work, their work within their product team, and their professional interactions. Our conversations suggest that AI is not only changing formal role responsibilities and collaborations between those roles, but also changing informal cultural practices like professional mentoring that are key to helping professionals settle in their positions, stay engaged with their work, and grow their careers. Some of these changes are positive, such as smoother collaboration between peers, but other changes are more nuanced and put the typical career growth opportunities, like receiving feedback from professional networks and promoting leadership and mentorship, at risk. We propose steps that AI companies can take to make the invisible work more visible. Additionally, we propose efforts that individuals and leaders can take to support their colleagues through AI transformation while preserving healthy company cultures that support diverse thinking, collaboration, and informal interactions.

</details>


### [279] [AMEL: Accumulated Message Effects on LLM Judgments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22714)
*Sid-ali Temkit*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 研究表明LLM作为评估器时会产生累积消息效应（AMEL）：先前对话的正负极性会显著影响后续判断。75,898次API调用验证了该效应稳健存在，效应量d=-0.17，负向历史产生1.62倍于正向的偏见。效应在不确定项目上更显著（d=-0.34），且不随上下文长度或位置变化。规模化可缓解但不能消除偏见，建议评估流程使用独立上下文或平衡历史极性。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 随着LLM被广泛用于代码审查、内容审核和输出评分等自动化评估任务，了解对话历史是否会影响模型判断的公正性变得至关重要。这关系到AI评估系统的可靠性和可信度。

动机：自动化评估中偏见的存在可能导致系统性错误，特别是在批量处理场景下。

Method: 采用2×2×11因子设计：75,898次API调用涵盖11个模型（来自OpenAI、Anthropic、Google及4个开源模型），将相同测试项目分别置于隔离状态、或跟随正/负面极性的饱和历史中。效应量以Cohen's d量化，统计检验采用t检验和回归分析。后续三个机制探查实验分别检验：概率分布的连续性变化、极性不对称的来源、以及位置效应。

方法：大规模跨模型实验设计，控制项目相同性，比较不同历史条件下的评分偏移。

Result: 1. 模型显著向对话 prevailing polarity 偏移（d=-0.17, p<10^-46）
2. 效应集中在高不确定性项目（d=-0.34）而非确定性项目（d=-0.15）
3. 上下文长度不影响效应强度（5轮与50轮产生相同偏移）
4. 负向极性产生1.62倍于正向的偏见（t=13.46, p<10^-39）
5. 模型规模有帮助但不消除偏见（Anthropic: Haiku -0.22→Opus -0.17; OpenAI: Nano -0.34→GPT-5.2 -0.17）
6. 位置无关：50轮对话中任意5轮偏见轮产生相同效应

结果：AMEL效应稳健存在，对高熵项目影响更大，负向偏见强于正向，且不受上下文长度和位置影响。

Conclusion: 上下文新鲜度是关键干预手段；当批量处理不可避免时，需平衡历史极性。规模化虽有助于减少偏差，但无法根除该问题，暗示这是LLM注意力机制的内在特性。

结论：LLM作为自动化评估器存在系统性偏见，建议在评估流程中使用干净上下文，或对批量样本进行极性平衡。

Abstract: Large language models are routinely used as automated evaluators: to review code, moderate content, or score outputs, often with many items passing through one conversation. We ask whether the polarity of prior conversation history biases subsequent judgments, an effect we call the accumulated message effect on LLM judgments (AMEL). Across 75,898 API calls to 11 models from 4 providers (OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, and four open-source models), we present identical test items in isolation or following histories saturated with predominantly positive or negative evaluations.
  Models shift toward the conversation's prevailing polarity (d = -0.17, p < 10^-46). The effect concentrates on items where the model is genuinely uncertain at baseline (d = -0.34 for high-entropy items, vs d = -0.15 when the baseline is deterministic). Bias does not grow with context length: 5 prior turns and 50 produce the same shift (Spearman |r| < 0.01; OLS slope p = 0.80). And there is a negativity asymmetry: paired per item, negative histories induce 1.62x more bias than positive (t = 13.46, p < 10^-39, n = 2,481). Scaling helps but does not solve it (Anthropic: Haiku -0.22 to Opus -0.17; OpenAI: Nano -0.34 to GPT-5.2 -0.17).
  Three follow-ups narrow the mechanism. The token probability distribution shifts continuously, not at a threshold. The negativity asymmetry has both token-level and semantic components, though attributing the balance is exploratory at our sample sizes. Position does not matter: five biased turns anywhere in a 50-turn history produce the same shift. The simplest fix for evaluation pipelines is a fresh context per item; when batching is unavoidable, balancing the history helps.

</details>


### [280] [Parametric Modular Answer Set Programs Made Declarative](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22716)
*Jorge Fandinno,Yuliya Lierler,Torsten Schaub*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: The paper proposes parametric modular logic programs as a new formalism to bring modularity to first-order ASP, successfully capturing clingo's collective control semantics and providing theoretical foundations connecting modular and non-modular ASP approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: First-order answer set programming lacks adequate support for modularity, making it difficult to structure, reuse, and instantiate subprograms in a systematic way.

Method: Introduced parametric modular logic programs formalism that supports subprogram parameters and intensionality statements, enabling formal capture of clingo-programs with collective control semantics.

Result: Demonstrated that parametric modular logic programs can capture the semantics of clingo-programs with collective control, establishing a formal bridge between modular and traditional ASP.

Conclusion: The paper establishes theoretical foundations for modular ASP, demonstrating the practicality of parametric modular logic programs and their connection to traditional non-modular ASP frameworks.

Abstract: In this paper, we explore the concept of modularity in first-order answer set programming (ASP). We introduce a new formalism called parametric modular logic programs, which allows defining subprograms with parameters and intensionality statements. We demonstrate how this formalism can capture the semantics of clingo-programs with collective control, a feature that enables structuring and instantiating subprograms. We provide theoretical foundations for modular ASP, illustrate its usefulness, and connect to traditional non-modular ASP.

</details>


### [281] [Towards a General Intelligence and Interface for Wearable Health Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22759)
*Girish Narayanswamy,Maxwell A. Xu,A. Ali Heydari,Samy Abdel-Ghaffar,Marius Guerard,Kara Vaillancourt,Zhihan Zhang,Jake Garrison,Levi Albuquerque,Dimitris Spathis,Hong Yu,Hamid Palangi,Xuhai "Orson" Xu,David G. T. Barrett,Joseph Breda,Jed McGiffin,Yubin Kim,Yuwei Zhang,Naghmeh Rezaei,Samuel Solomon,Karan Ahuja,Tim Althoff,Jake Sunshine,Ming-Zher Poh,Benjamin Yetton,Ari Winbush,Nicholas B. Allen,James M. Rehg,Isaac Galatzer-Levy,Yun Liu,John Hernandez,Anupam Pathak,Conor Heneghan,Yuzhe Yang,Ahmed A. Metwally,Pushmeet Kohli,Mark Malhotra,Shwetak Patel,Xin Liu,Daniel McDuff*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Google-scale wearable health foundation model trained on 5M participants achieves broad health prediction capabilities via population-scale pretraining, enabling few-shot adaptation and LLM-driven model selection, with clinical validation of 1,860 ratings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Wearable sensors struggle to transform low-level data into meaningful health insights due to phenotypic diversity, individual baseline variation, and scarcity of high-quality labeled data for supervised learning.

Method: Foundation model pretrained on 1 trillion+ minutes of unlabeled sensor data from 5M participants; LLM agent classroom for autonomous downstream head architecture search; Personal Health Agent integration for clinical validation.

Result: Systematic performance improvements across 35 health prediction tasks; label-efficient few-shot learning; LLM agent performance scales with model capacity; 1,860 clinician ratings validate Personal Health Agent utility and safety.

Conclusion: Population-scale pretraining produces generalizable health representations that enable label-efficient downstream adaptation, autonomous LLM-driven model selection, and clinically validated Personal Health Agents for wearable data.

Abstract: While ubiquitous wearable sensors capture a wealth of behavioral and physiological information, effectively transforming these signals into personalized health insights is challenging. Specifically, converting low-level sensor data into representations capable of characterizing higher-level states is difficult due to high phenotypic diversity and variation in individual baseline health, physiology, and lifestyle factors. Moreover, collecting wearable data paired with health outcome annotations is laborious and expensive, and retrospective annotation remains practically unfeasible, contributing to a scarcity of data with high-quality labels. To overcome these limitations, we propose a foundation model for wearable health that is pretrained on more than one trillion minutes of unlabeled sensor signals drawn from a large cohort of five million participants. We demonstrate that the joint scaling of model capacity and pretraining data volume leads to systematic improvements in performance, as evaluated on a diverse set of 35 health prediction tasks, spanning cardiovascular, metabolic, sleep, and mental health, as well as lifestyle choices and demographic factors. We find that this population scale representation unlocks label-efficient few-shot learning and generative capabilities for robust daily metric estimation. To further leverage this learned representation, we deploy a classroom of LLM agents to autonomously search the space of downstream predictive heads built on the model embeddings, showing broad performance improvements that increase with LLM model capacity. Finally, we show how integrating these downstream predictors into a Personal Health Agent can support model responses that are more relevant, contextually aware, and safe, and we validate this via 1,860 ratings from a cohort of clinicians.

</details>


### [282] [Advancing Mathematics Research with AI-Driven Formal Proof Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22763)
*George Tsoukalas,Anton Kovsharov,Sergey Shirobokov,Anja Surina,Moritz Firsching,Gergely Bérczi,Francisco J. R. Ruiz,Arun Suggala,Adam Zsolt Wagner,Eric Wieser,Lei Yu,Aja Huang,Miklós Z. Horváth,Andrew Ferrauiolo,Henryk Michalewski,Codrut Grosu,Thomas Hubert,Matej Balog,Pushmeet Kohli,Swarat Chaudhuri*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: First large-scale evaluation of LLMs solving open mathematical problems via formal proof in Lean; demonstrated AI can autonomously solve ~2.5% of open Erdős problems and ~9% of OEIS conjectures, with applications across multiple mathematical domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LLMs show promise in mathematical reasoning but unreliability limits research utility; formal proof generation in languages like Lean offers a mitigation strategy

Method: Large-scale evaluation of LLM agents generating formal proofs in Lean; comparison of capable agent vs basic agent alternating between LLM-based generation and Lean-based verification

Result: Capable agent autonomously resolved 9/353 open Erdős problems, proved 44/492 OEIS conjectures; basic agent replicated Erdős successes but at higher cost on hardest problems

Conclusion: Agent design with LLM-based generation and Lean-based verification effectively enables AI-aided formal proof search for open mathematical problems. More capable agents achieved better cost-efficiency on hardest problems.

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) increasingly excel at mathematical reasoning, but their unreliability limits their utility in mathematics research. A mitigation is using LLMs to generate formal proofs in languages like Lean. We perform the first large-scale evaluation of this method's ability to solve open problems. Our most capable agent autonomously resolved 9 of 353 open Erdős problems at the per-problem cost of a few hundred dollars, proved 44/492 OEIS conjectures, and is being deployed in combinatorics, optimization, graph theory, algebraic geometry, and quantum optics research. A basic agent alternating LLM-based generation with Lean-based verification replicated the Erdős successes but proved costlier on the hardest problems. These findings demonstrate the power of AI-aided formal proof search and shed light on the agent designs that enable it.

</details>


### [283] [Gated DeltaNet-2: Decoupling Erase and Write in Linear Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22791)
*Ali Hatamizadeh,Yejin Choi,Jan Kautz*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Gated DeltaNet-2 removes the scalar gate limitation in prior delta-rule linear attention by introducing independent channel-wise erase and write gates, achieving state-of-the-art results among linear attention variants at 1.3B parameters, especially on long-context retrieval tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
